The released body already goes to bodies.json, which the next full pass reads as previous; both workdirs hold one kept_bodies row per tool, the shape a full pass leaves, so nothing was discarded. And no repair ruling on either workdir said "cleared the gates": 34 rulings on one and 48 on the other read "still assisted", and the phrase in the reports is the assisted red light's own wording. What the ruling never said is what the attempt scored. On telecom one tool scored 12 against the kept 104 with no crash: every gate up to replay_fidelity passed and all 144 shown calls answered. The kept body writes the device status line as a literal; the new one derives it from four columns of the device row the seeded world fills differently. The score rewards the memorised constant and the ruling gave the Builder no way to see it.

Three rules. The repair ruling carries the score pair, which body was released, how much evidence a replay failure put back, and whether the tool is stalled. A recorded call the last Reference replay failed on is evidence for that tool whatever the after-write skip (D74), the anchor hold-out (D81) or the caller filter (D164) says, because those answer what a body may be written from and this answers what a Task has to reproduce; the lesson names the column and the two values, masked to a count for any call the writer is not shown. And a recompile that scores no higher counts against the tool; the count survives a full pass, and from the second the ruling and the red light read "stalled: N recompiles in a row scored no higher".

Measured on the copies, code only, no model call, no spend. The evidence a replay failure puts back is small and nearly all anchor: on one airline write tool 7 calls over an evidence set of 36 (3 after-write, 4 anchor); on one telecom device tool 3 over 216, all anchor. That disagrees with the premise that the filters hide the failures: 15 of 22 and 56 of 59 failing calls were already scored. The gain is the lesson, which now names the differing column, and the honest ruling. The cost is that D81 no longer holds for those few calls, and that compile_tools declares replay_evidence.json, so the pass after the first replay recompiles once and then settles. Nineteen tests on invented domains. On the next builds read from_replay and unbeaten per tool in kept_bodies.json, and whether a stalled tool stops drawing rounds.

### D193. The judge's residue gets a second pass or an abstention (2026-09-08)

The founder asked to observe from the errors and fix things generally, without overfitting (2026-09-08). The funnel read of the same day says the largest single reason a Task that replayed keeps no Reference is the judge ruling and two or more End states still disagreeing: 28 Tasks on the first corpus, 11 on the second and 2 on the third, of 113 that pass fidelity and are not trusted, with the judge failing every End state another 9 and 2. The judge is fail-only by design (D110), so with three or more states in front of it it can drop some and still leave a residue one pass cannot settle.

Decided. A judgement that leaves two or more states in is asked once more over those survivors alone, with the keys they differ on recomputed over them and a stop rule saying exactly one may remain: fail every other survivor with a D186 citation, or say the survivors cannot be told apart, an abstention whose reason names those keys. One extra call per residue Task, judge_passes 2 on the reference row with the second reply kept the way the first is, and judge_residue_resolved and judge_residue_abstained per round. A second pass that still leaves two or more, or whose failure cites nothing, abstains the same way. When instead every state is failed, the failures are read against each other: agreeing on one key and one required value from the Intent or a numbered policy line, they are one claim that no recording did what the Task required, recorded as no_correct_recording with that key, a reason of its own that KIND_OF_RULING maps for a later Examiner beat to file; disagreeing among themselves, they abstain as failed all on differing grounds. A pass still needs no citation, D185's opt-in agent takes its second pass through the same judge_groups dispatch, and CACHE_FORMAT is bumped.

Seven tests on invented domains: a residue resolved by a cited second pass, an uncited one, one that fails nothing, failed all on agreeing and on disagreeing citations, judge_passes on the row, and the agent asked twice.

Measured read only on a copy of the second corpus's live workdir, both arms in one process over one judge, References only and no D79 check. Of its 20 residue and failed-all Tasks the second pass was asked on 13 and resolved none: all 13 took the escape hatch the stop rule offers, so References stayed 3 of 20 and agreement with the corpus reward 1 of 2 kept, before and after alike. Three read as no_correct_recording. 0.038 USD, the first pass coming back memoised and only the second a fresh call. The same run over the first corpus's 37 Tasks was stopped under load before it judged any, so that corpus has no number.

The premise did not hold. A residue read as a judge not yet asked the last question; the numbers say it is a judge that will not commit, which is what D185 and D186 already found. What ships is the abstention and the reading of a failed-all judgement, both of which say what happened, not a gain in References. Next to read is judge_residue_resolved against judge_residue_abstained per round, and the no_correct_recording Tasks, the first candidates for an Environment or Intent finding rather than another re-roll.

### D194. False rejection over the held-out pool gates trusted (2026-09-08)

The founder asked for fixes read off the errors that hold generally ("observe from the errors and fix things generally, it should not overfit", 2026-09-08). The funnel read over the three live builds found this one inside a ruling: the trusted ruling measured D133's false-rejection number for every Task, wrote it into its own metrics, and never read it in its reason chain. Three Tasks on the first corpus (task_c280d4c61cef, task_6c7a19337b05, task_12bf34ff4998) counted as trusted while their required atoms rejected every held-out Run that had reached the Reference, two more did on the second (task_7cc03b8bfc5c, task_d14a4f6c7cb3), and on the third no Verifier passed the suite at all. The same measure fails a Verifier in the false-rejection gate and raises a finding for the Examiner, so two rulings disagreed about one Verifier.

The rule: a Task is trusted only when its false rejection over the held-out pool is under D133's threshold, or the pool is empty, in which case the step records no_pool and the other checks decide. A Verifier that turns away every Run which reached the Reference checks one path, not the Task, so counting it as trusted overstated the number the loop is aimed at. The threshold now lives in one place, FALSE_REJECTION_THRESHOLD in the loosening module with over_strict as the predicate over a row, read there by the false-rejection gate, the finding and the trusted ruling alike. Every ruling carries the fraction, the pool size and that per-Task word, so a rate is never reported without its denominator.

Three tests cover it on the gates suite's invented domain: a fraction at the threshold is not trusted and the failure names it with the fraction and the pool size, a fraction under it is trusted, and an empty pool says no_pool and leaves the Task to the other checks.

Measured offline on copies of the two workdirs holding trusted Tasks, code only, no model call, spend zero. Trusted falls from 119 to 116 over 200 Verifiers on the first corpus, from 28 to 26 over 61 on the second, and from 0 to 0 on the third: an honest loss of five, not a regression.

Two numbers cut against the premise that this gate does much work. Of the 70 Tasks on the first corpus holding a pool, 23 sit at the threshold, but 20 already failed the D79 suite, so the step decides three; on the second, 5 of 12 sit there and 2 are new. And the pool is empty for 130 of 200 Tasks on the first corpus and 49 of 61 on the second, so the gate is silent on most of both until re-rolls and live traces arrive. D185's filter of the pool down to the Runs that reached the Reference now has an effect it did not have before: until today it moved a reported number and no decision.

gates_version is a hash of the package, so these two files moving moves the version the ruling carries, and the three workdirs need freezing again before the next launch. Next to read is false_rejection_pool beside false_rejection per round, whether the pool grows, and whether the flipped Tasks come back once the Examiner acts on the finding raised for them.

### D195. A body that reads state answers differently when that state differs (2026-09-08)

The founder asked again to "observe from the errors and fix things generally, it should not overfit" (2026-09-08). The idea comes from Repo2RLEnv: a synthesized test counts only once it fails on a stub and passes on the real thing. Kullback asks that of a Verifier (D79), never of a tool body, which passes whenever its answers match the recordings, read or memorised. D191 watched a kept body write a status line as a constant and beat an attempt that derived it from the row.

compile_tools gains an eighth ruling, sensitivity, between non_trivial and replay_fidelity. Two recorded calls of one tool, under two Tasks, whose arguments match or differ only in the row they name, and whose recorded results part in a column, are a sensitivity pair; a column holding those two row ids is dropped, since echoing an argument is not reading a world. A column counts only where the two Task worlds hold it differently; where they hold one value the fault is an unpinned Starting state, which replay already blocks. The body must part in one of those columns; answering both alike is memorising or reading the wrong row. The failure names the tool, the column and the two Task ids, never a value; the lesson names the columns. A pair parting in the whole answer, where the tool has no reader (D176), becomes unread_pairs and is not ruled on; no pair passes as no_pairs. This gate alone does not stop the chain: it runs before replay_fidelity so gates passed lifts a reading body over a memorising one in attempt_score and keep-by-beating (D184), and the chain goes on so the replay count still breaks ties among failing bodies.

Seventeen tests on an invented plant nursery cover a constant body, a reading body, one reading another Task's row, a column both worlds hold alike, an exempt column, a nested row id and an unread prose result.

Measured on copies of two live workdirs, no model call. Telecom: 36 tools, 48 ruled pairs over 13 tools, 10 unread pairs over 3 more, 23 with no pair, one failing, and real: that body tests a device column against the string on while the two pinned worlds hold Yes and No. Airline: 13 tools, 5 ruled pairs over 2 tools, nothing failing.

Two numbers cut against the premise. Without the test that the worlds hold the column, the rule failed 6 telecom tools and 2 airline tools, seven of those eight parting in a column neither world holds, so the accusation belonged to the Starting state. And rejecting a body wins no fidelity by itself: dropped with nothing in its place, telecom falls from 19 of 329 Tasks confirmed to 17 and writes matched from 2786 to 2617, airline is unchanged at 88 of 122. That is the floor: a live stage recompiles with the lesson, which cannot be measured offline.

The cost is one more ruling per attempt with no subprocess, a pair search capped at 25 pairs and four worlds per bucket, and two files under kullback/gates moved, so gates_version moves and the workdirs need freezing again. On the next builds read pairs, unread_pairs and no_pairs per tool: a corpus mostly no_pairs is one this gate cannot see.
### D196. Intents are written symptom-only, and the leak check audits the strip (2026-09-08)

The founder asked for fixes read off the errors that hold generally ("observe from the errors and fix things generally, it should not overfit", 2026-09-08), pointing at huggingface/Repo2RLEnv, which strips leaks out of the text that becomes a task's instruction and audits the rest. Kullback had the audit and not the strip: the D79 leak check fails a whole Task whose Intent states a value only the system knew, and the funnel read counted 3 such Tasks on one corpus and 6 on another.

The rule. Before a candidate line is graded, every value the recording's own tools held and no user of any Run of the Task said is taken out of it. canon.class_of, the lookup the compare reads a column's class through, classifies it and leaves one of three shapes: a code leaves its last four characters, a date its month, and an amount, a name or an exempt column nothing. The pass is code and never reads a value it wrote itself, so stripping twice is stripping once. A model rewrite is allowed only where the strip leaves a line that no longer reads: the miner's next attempt, told which columns went and not their values, stripped again. The Intent record carries what was taken as a column, a class, a shape and a source, never a value, and the words a shape leaves are exempt from grounding, since no Run can evidence them. The leak check reads that record: it fails as it always did and names the column of every value the strip missed, which the status row carries as leak_columns and findings.py files per Task and column (D192). The ratchet keeps a recorded line only when the strip would not change it. The strip reads the recording, not the whole database, since a Starting state reaches an Intent only through what it read.

Tests on an invented bike hire domain: a value only the tools held is taken out and the record names its column and class but no value, a value the rider said stays, a code becomes its last characters, a stripped line still grounds, and a line written before the strip does not hold. The leak check names the column of what was missed, filed per Task and column.

Measured offline on copies of both workdirs, code only, spend zero. On the first corpus the strip touches 12 of 213 Intents and takes 13 values, 8 as last four characters and 5 removed, all hard columns; the leak check over its 201 Verifiers falls from 5 failing Tasks to 0, and all 12 stripped lines still ground. On the second it touches 0 of 125 Intents and fails 0 of 78 Verifiers either way. Two numbers cut against the premise: the second corpus does not move at all, and the funnel read's 6 airline leak failures are not in that workdir, a snapshot of a running build.

The frozen packages moved, so gates_version and the three workdirs need freezing again. Next to read is intents_stripped and values_stripped beside leak_misses in the round counts: a corpus where the first two are zero and the third is not has a strip reading different values from its check.

### D198. A residue is settled by deriving a Verifier per surviving End state, and a suite failure names its checks (2026-09-08)

The founder asked to observe from the errors and fix things generally, without overfitting (2026-09-08). The airline funnel that day showed fourteen Tasks that replayed and kept no Reference because the judgement left two or more End states in and abstained. D185, D186 and D193 each measured that judge and found the same, D193's arm settling none of its thirteen residues: it is asked a question it will not answer. Separately, twenty-five Tasks stopped at the D79 suite behind a reason naming none of its nine checks, so a reader could not group them.

The first rule stops asking. A judgement leaving two or more End states in, or failing every state on grounds that do not agree, hands those states to the derivation: one Verifier per survivor, derived as if it were the Reference, by the same path and atoms, and put through the D79 suite and D133's false rejection. The survivor whose Verifier passes most checks with the lowest false rejection is the Reference, survivor_chosen with the counts. Survivors equal on both are equal under everything the harness can ask, so the Task takes the first by recording order as survivors_equivalent, D184's keep by beating applied to References. Where none stands up the Task keeps no Reference, the reason naming the best as survivors_all_fail. Four survivors per Task at most, survivors_capped above that. Nothing buys a Run: the derivation is code and the loophole probe is left to the released Verifier, so candidates are compared on the checks all can be asked, the winner still facing the whole suite. It fires only where a judgement was made: with no judge nothing was asked, and D111 keeps no Reference for recordings that disagree. The second rule: a suite failure names every failing check and every check with no input, each with its own gate's reason, in the suite's fixed order.

Eleven tests on invented domains cover the three answers, the tie, the cap, the round counts and a suite failure naming its checks.

Measured offline on copies of two live workdirs, code only, no model call, zero of the two USD allowed. Airline, 122 Tasks: References 70 before and 85 after, over 16 residue Tasks, 12 chosen, 3 equivalent, 1 all failing. Retail, 205 Tasks: 152 before and 179 after over 29 residue Tasks, 20 chosen, 7 equivalent, 2 all failing, and 187 with the 9 whose judgement failed every state, which that older record cannot split by agreeing and differing grounds. Two numbers cut against the premise. The gap is usually wide rather than fine: the loser commonly fails empty_fails, plausible_wrong_fails and unsolved_state_fails together, a Verifier derived from a Run that wrote nothing. The false rejection decides almost nothing, the pool being empty for most survivors: one Task on airline, none on retail. Verifiers passing and trusted after are not measured: the winner's suite still needs the loophole probe and second path, which cost Runs.

Two frozen files moved, kullback/gates/trust.py and kullback/gates/verifier_suite.py, so the three workdirs need freezing again and CACHE_FORMAT is bumped. Next to read is residue_derived against the three outcomes per round, whether chosen survivors pass the suite, and the survivors_all_fail Tasks.

### D199. When re-rolls find no second path, one is written from the Reference Run (2026-09-08)

The founder asked again to observe from the errors and fix generally, without overfitting (2026-09-08). Check 5 of the D79 suite asks whether the Verifier accepts a different way to the same End state; D189 buys one as fresh Runs. Round 1 of the airline build after D187 to D194 prices it: second_path_found 6 at 126 Runs over 42 batches against second_path_exhausted 12, and 6 of D189's 13 lone Tasks spent all three batches. On a copy of that workdir, 15 of the 70 Tasks with a confirmed Reference had none. Many Tasks have one path, so the search paid a model to fail.

The derivation writes one instead. When the buying is spent, the Reference Run's call path is rewritten three ways, each leaving the world where it was: two adjacent calls of one speaker neither of which reads a row the other wrote are swapped, a read the Run already made is repeated before a write, and a read whose answer no later call's arguments carry is left out. Independence is judged on the rows the calls touched, read off the recorded arguments and results, where a row id is a value under a key named like an identifier at any depth; a refused write wrote nothing (D67), so it touches rows and writes none. Each rewrite is replayed from the Starting state on the path replay fidelity uses, as a Builder callable so the Examiner reads no body (D123), and kept only where it reaches the Reference's End state under the D111 and D182 rule. A Run offering no rewrite is single by structure, an honest end state; that not run stops blocking that Task alone, and only where every other check passes and the held-out pool holds a Run that reached the Reference. The round counts second_path_synthesised, single_path_by_structure, synth_variants_tried, synth_variants_kept and second_path_waived.

Twenty tests on an invented greenhouse cover a reordered independent pair, a dependent pair and two writes on one row left alone, a read inserted before a write, a read a later argument used not dropped, a rewrite landing elsewhere not kept, and a single path unblocked where the pool holds a Run and blocking otherwise.

Measured on a copy of the airline workdir, code only, spend zero. Of its 15 lone Tasks, 13 offer a rewrite and two hold a Reference whose file was missing. Seventy rewrites were tried and seventy kept. Against the Verifiers on disk, check 5 passes on a written path for 7 of the 13, and Tasks clearing the whole suite go 46 to 53 of 70 confirmed; trusted was not re-measured, the false-rejection ruling needing a round. Three things disagree with the premise. This corpus has no Task without a rewrite, so single_path_by_structure and its waiver ship tested but unseen live. Every rewrite that replayed reached the End state, so the keeping rule rejects little, and reordering two reads is a weak second path where a Verifier's atoms are all writes and stated facts. And the 6 Tasks that gain nothing fail check 2 too, so a second path was never their blocker. Next builds should read synth_variants_kept against synth_variants_tried and whether a Task passing on a written path stays trusted.
### D200. The frozen Task list is authoritative across rounds (2026-09-08)

The founder asked for fixes read off the errors that hold generally ("observe from the errors and fix things generally, it should not overfit", 2026-09-08). The error was read off the first corpus on its relaunch: the workdir froze 119 Tasks and its live list now holds 122, with 3 frozen Tasks gone and 6 new ones in the same three categories the gone ones sat in. No new recording arrived. The cache moved under D188, so intents and starting_state were rebuilt, the world each Run started in was mined again, and the split by starting world grouped the same Runs differently. The second corpus, measured for this entry, had the same drift and nobody had noticed: 205 frozen, 209 live, 4 frozen Tasks gone, 8 new, and one of them was a Task the last trusted ruling had counted. Every headline number is counted over the frozen list, so a Task that moves takes its ruling, its cache and the comparability of two rounds with it.

The rule has two halves. A Task's id is now content-addressed over the sorted Run ids it groups and nothing else. It used to hash the Category id beside them, and the Category id is the write-tool signature, so re-mining one tool from read to write moved every id in that Category even where the same Runs were grouped the same way. A Run belongs to exactly one Task, so the Run set names the Task on its own. The second half is resumption: once a workdir holds a frozen list, that list is the Task list. A frozen Task keeps its id, its Runs and its Category; a Run the frozen list does not hold is free to form a new Task, which is how a corpus grows; a Run the frozen list does hold stays where it was even when the rebuild would have moved it, and the freshly grouped Task it was going to join keeps only the Runs nobody had frozen, under the id those Runs address. A frozen Task the rebuild would have removed is kept and marked frozen_only with its reason. The count of those rides on the round as tasks_frozen, tasks_added and tasks_frozen_only, printed on the round line, so drift is a finding for the Examiner rather than a quietly different denominator. Because the list has to be resumed and not just counted, the frozen file now stores the Task record beside the id, at format 2; a file written as ids alone is filled in from the per-Task files the cluster stage wrote beside it, and a frozen Task is matched to the rebuild on its Run set as well as on its id, so a list frozen by the older harness resumes without its ids moving.

Eight tests on the clustering suite's invented domain: the same Runs give the same id in two clusterings and keep it when a tool is re-mined from read to write into another Category; a second clustering over a frozen list keeps every frozen Task and adds none; a new Run adds one Task and moves no frozen one; a re-cluster that merges two frozen Tasks publishes neither the merge nor a loss and marks both frozen_only; a merge that swallows one frozen Run keeps the frozen Task and adds a Task holding only the free Run; with no frozen list every Task is new; and an id from before content addressing is matched on its Runs. Three more cover the frozen file: it stores the records, it fills an ids-only file in from the per-Task files, and a build that never froze reads back as no list at all. One on the driver checks a round carries the three counts.

Measured code only on copies of two live workdirs, no model call, spend zero. First corpus: frozen 119, rebuild 122, after the rule 119 published, 0 added, 3 frozen_only, and all 5 trusted ids of the last round present. Second corpus: frozen 205, rebuild 209, after 205 published, 0 added, 4 frozen_only, and all 119 trusted ids present where the live list on disk had already lost one of them. The number that cuts against the premise is added 0 on both: no Run in either corpus is outside the frozen list, so today the rule only holds Tasks in place and the growth half of it is untested against a real corpus. It also does not repair the split. The 3 and the 4 frozen_only Tasks say the world mining still groups those Runs differently between rounds, which is the D188 class of problem and stays open; this decision makes that visible and stops it moving the denominator.

The frozen packages moved: kullback/gates/scorecard.py gained the record-carrying frozen file and its reader, and gates/__init__ exports it, so gates_version moves and the three workdirs need freezing again before the next launch. Next to read is tasks_frozen_only on the round line of each build: it should be zero once mining is deterministic across rounds, and any build where it stays above zero names the Tasks whose Runs the split cannot hold still.

### D201. A repair lands only if it fixed what it targeted and cost nothing elsewhere (2026-09-08)

The founder asked to learn from pi.dev and others on how to improve the harness, and the harness design survey of 2026-09-08 ranked second an acceptance and regression gate every harness edit passes before it lands. Kullback had that for tool bodies alone: D184 keeps a body unless the attempt scores higher, D191 records the score pair. Every other repair landed on its own ruling and nothing asked what else had moved. Read on copies of two live workdirs' record files, code only: the first build made 103 repair requests over four rounds, 51 of them recompiles, 25 of those leaving the body byte for byte as it was; the second made 188 over nine rounds, 151 recompiles, 82 changing nothing. Of those recompiles 43 of 51 and 141 of 151 went to tools still assisted, and 32 Intent repairs went to 12 Tasks, 9 repaired more than once. Nothing said a repair had bought nothing, so the same target drew round after round.

Decided. Every acting repair is a transaction, one function every repair tool goes through. Before the stage runs, the Tasks the repair can touch are worked out, one Task for an Intent, the Tasks a tool's recorded calls belong to for a body, the whole corpus otherwise, and the four lights each stands on are recorded: replay, Reference, suite, trusted. After the stage they are read again off the artifacts it wrote. The repair is accepted only where its own target moved up and no Task in the set lost a light; otherwise the files that kind writes are put back byte for byte and the ruling reads reverted, naming the Tasks and the light they lost, or no effect where the target did not move. That sentence leads the tool result, so the next attempt reads what the last one cost. Rounds count repairs_accepted, repairs_reverted_regression, repairs_reverted_no_effect and repairs_deferred, and the round report lists the reverted repairs by kind. The body scorer is one instance and not a second path: its set is the tool's own recorded calls, its target state is the score, and an attempt ahead on the score and behind on the calls is now reverted, which the score alone could not see. A kind that cannot be scoped is held against the whole corpus three times in a round; the fourth applies with its check deferred to the round's own gates. No replay is bought, the scoped replay being the one the repairing stage already ran, and the stage's own record is stamped rather than put back, because the score pair is the lesson the next hint answers.

Twelve tests on an invented domain of seed trays cover the three outcomes, the body scorer through the same function, the scoping, the deferred fourth, and the revert.

The numbers disagree with half the premise. No Task lost the trusted or replay light in any closed round of either build, trusted flat at 119 over three rounds on the first and rising from 39 to 44 over two on the second, so the regression half ships on the argument and not on a measurement. The other half is large: half the first build's recompiles and more than half the second's moved nothing. The cost was nothing, no model call and no spend. Next read repairs_accepted against repairs_reverted_no_effect per round, the reverted line in the round report, and the reverted and broke fields on the kept_bodies rows.
### D202. A column a Task only ever writes is pinned by running the write's own body backwards (2026-09-08)

The founder asked to fix generally, without overfitting (2026-09-08). One corpus's second fidelity reading put 133 of its 329 Tasks on a starting value seeded to a shape the Task's own recording never agreed with, the largest cause there; another carries it smaller at 38 of 122. D188 pins a column from the Task's earliest sighting, which needs a result to state it. A column first touched by a write is never sighted before it, so the pinner read nothing and the column kept the corpus wide seed, though the write's recorded result is evidence of what it held.

The rule is that the released body is run backwards. Every write call of a Task, from any requestor and not only the assistant, is replayed against the world D188 pinned; those reproducing their recording are left alone and the rest are the evidence. The rows a failing call could have touched are taken in order: rows its result states, rows its arguments name, its reader table's rows in the Task, then all of them. Columns are tried fewest distinct corpus values first, and skipped when a key, when the Task read the column before the write, or when the readers stage filled it rather than read it. Candidates are the values the corpus gave that column, most frequent first, capped at sixteen, one call worth sixty four tries. A candidate is kept when the tool's body run on the world holding it produces a result the D187 comparison calls the same as the recording. The first match wins, the value goes into the Task's overlay before it is hashed so `call_starting_states` serves it unchanged, and an assumption names the tool, call and body hash. A column with no inverse is left as D188 left it, and counted.

The bodies are the input and the Starting state runs above the stage releasing them, so it reads `bodies.json` from the workdir and declares it an input path: the first build inverts nothing, later builds do, and a recompile that moves a body re runs the inversion. Three cache tests now say the Starting state is cached one build later.

Twelve tests on an invented domain of meters and seals cover a flag and an enum pinned by inversion, no candidate counted per tool, a column read before its write, a write the pre state reproduces, two writes of one column, the pin reaching `call_starting_states`, no body yet, another requestor's write, and a corpus filled column treated as unread.

Measured on copies, code only, zero cost. On the corpus the reading came from, 183 Tasks, 51 columns inverted and 1116 with no inverse over 4972 candidates, the first failure moving off toggle_roaming for 7 Tasks and check_app_permissions for 3. On the other, 122 Tasks, nothing inverted, 35 with no inverse over 320 candidates, an identical reason table. Both disagree with the premise on the number that matters: Tasks confirmed stayed 17 and 54, because an inversion moves a Task's first failure later rather than removing it, so can_send_mms rose from 13 to 19 and check_network_status from 10 to 15 as earlier calls began passing. Next builds: read columns_inverted against columns_no_inverse per tool in `overlay_pins.json`, and whether a Task whose first write now replays fails later or out of turn order.

### D203. A tool whose prose nobody reads gets a reader of its own (2026-09-08)

The founder asked to observe from the errors and fix things generally, without overfitting. The
error is a hole in D176: readers are proposed one requestor toolkit at a time, so a tool of the
customer's own system that answers a sentence gets no reader, and D180 homing has nothing to home.
The airline recording left 112 homed prose results of one read tool unread and homed no column at
all; the telecom recording left 231 results of two tools unread.

The rule is that after the readers stage every tool with homed prose results no reader answers is a
gap the harness closes itself. The tool's own recorded results are aligned into templates: tokenise
on whitespace, group by token width, the positions every result agrees on are the literal frame and
the rest are slots. A slot binds to the one column of the homed row whose value equals it on every
result, exactly first and then through canon. A bound template compiles to a reader and a render,
kept only where the D183 round trip reproduces every recorded result character for character from
the row the corpus held. Where a slot binds to no column or to two, one model call per tool asks
for the pair, given the templates, the masked slot values and the row's columns, and the same round
trip rules on it. That reader may also name a column the row does not hold, which is what the read
reveals about the row. A reveal has no column to check it against, so it is held to two other
things: one row answers one value for it within a run, or the sentence states something varying
under the row rather than a column of it; and it takes more than one value over the corpus, or it
asserts nothing. A revealed column enters the schema marked by the tool that revealed it, because
the tool bodies are written from the schema. A tool answering one string to every call is a
constant acknowledgement, counted apart, and results still unread are counted per tool and filed as
one environment finding naming their masked shapes.

Twenty one tests over an invented courier depot cover alignment, binding, forcing, the rejected
render, the revealed column entering the schema, both refusals of a reveal, and unread results
counted by shape.

Measured offline on workdir copies with D197 merged in. Airline went from 88 of 122 Tasks confirmed
to 100, columns homed from 0 to 255, sequences served from 0 to 52, and the read that blocked
thirty Tasks stopped disagreeing. Telecom did not move, 19 of 329 either way: one unread tool
answers one string to 164 calls, the other has two results, below the three an alignment needs, and
its forced proposal failed the round trip. The disagreement to state plainly is that step one, the
code only half, derived nothing on either corpus: every gain came from the one forced call, so the
alignment is proven by tests and not yet by a corpus. Adding one column to the airline schema also
moved the mined counts, because the miner reads a result differently once its table holds it. Cost
was four forced calls, far under a dollar. Read readers_derived, readers_forced, columns_revealed,
slots_unbound and results_unread on the next builds.

### D204. The replay cursor takes a run of consecutive same-role turns as one logical turn (2026-09-08)

The founder asked again to observe from the errors and fix things generally, it should not overfit (2026-09-08). One live workdir had 427 of 456 Traces reporting turns out of order and 70 of its 329 Tasks blocked on that line. The shape was always the same: the assistant replies in text, the user answers with a tool call of its own, then a second user turn stands before the assistant speaks.

The cursor handed the loop one turn per ask, in lockstep with the loop's rule of model, then user once, repeat. Where one side speaks twice before the other answers, that handoff stalls and the two never resync, so every later turn is a gap and every later write never replays. It is a class: it appears wherever the user side can act, an application calling its own tool or a person sending two messages before an answer.

The rule. The cursor takes the whole run of consecutive turns of the asked role as one logical turn, reading past tool turns as it already did. That side is handed what those turns said, in order with a newline between them, and every call they made; a user turn's own calls are routed before the next assistant ask and compared like any other recorded call, named user_call in the reasons. A run closes at a turn that called a tool only for the role the loop comes back to by itself, which is the model alone, since the loop asks the model again while its reply carries calls and asks the user once for a reply carrying none; without that, a model turn separated from the next by its own tool result would be swallowed and the ask the user is owed would never happen. A gap is counted only where the turn at the cursor is of neither the asked role nor a run the cursor can absorb. Counts gain absorbed_user_runs, absorbed_model_runs and absorbed_turns, the stage summary gains turns_absorbed, and the live loop is untouched.

Six tests on an invented order domain: two user turns in a row are one turn and the write after them replays; a tool called inside a user run is routed in order and compared; a user call that parts is named user_call; two assistant turns in a row are one turn and the user is asked once; a real mismatch counts one gap and then resyncs; alternating roles absorb nothing.

Measured offline on copies of three live workdirs, code only, no model call and no spend. Telecom moves from 19 of 329 Tasks confirmed to 90, Traces with a gap from 427 to 0, gaps from 4319 to 0, with 3224 user runs and 4918 turns absorbed; 224 of the 239 Tasks still unconfirmed now rank a tool the user called itself, over twelve tools, where before those calls were reported under the assistant's labels. Airline and retail are unchanged, 88 of 122 and 203 of 209, turns_absorbed zero.

That zero is the disagreement: only the corpus the observation came from moved, because neither other corpus records such a run, so they show the rule costs nothing where the shape is absent, not that it wins anything. Two frozen files changed, kullback/runner/replay.py and kullback/gates/fidelity.py, so the three workdirs need freezing again; the stage versions itself off the module, so replays.json rebuilds with no constant to bump. On the next builds read turns_absorbed, which says whether a corpus has this shape, and the user_call reasons, which name tool bodies no reading had separated before.
### D205. An over-strict Verifier is loosened by the harness itself, gated as any repair (2026-09-08)

The loosening read found a rule with three readers and no actor: over_strict, the predicate D194 put in one place, is read by the false-rejection gate, the trusted ruling and the finding rule, and none acts. The one verb that could relax a Verifier is the Examiner's repair, which a model must choose, and the first live workdir never chose it: 24 false-rejection findings over 23 Tasks, all closed without one, the fraction at 1.0 every round. It holds 206 Verifiers today, 32 with a held-out pool and 19 over_strict; the second holds 85, 9 pooled and 6.

The rule. After a Task is ruled over_strict the derivation runs the loosening step itself, code only, over the first held-out Run that reached the Reference End state and the Verifier rejects. An atom that turns such a Run away while the End state it guards is satisfied is over-specific by definition, and is relaxed by kind, never by name. A question atom is dropped, because it pins the order one recording took, and so is an atom over a tool that writes nothing. A write atom naming a row becomes a wrote-any over the same tool, the End state it implies; a write value becomes D190's shape atom, the column without its literal; an answer atom becomes D190's reported fact, same predicate, rejecting no Run. Anything else stops the proposal rather than being guessed at, so a write cap leaves the Task its finding. Two relaxations per proposal at most, one per Task per round, two rounds, then it is the Examiner's. It goes through propose_version, the repair tool's path lifted out of it, so the D79 suite, the probe pool and one-directional loosening rule on it; a rejection leaves the Task as it was under auto_loosen_rejected naming the check, an acceptance closes the finding. Two frozen files moved: verifier_suite reads a write atom naming no row as one about the tool, in its scorer and its extra-write check, as the Runner always did; records gained auto_loosen as a proposer. D192's gap closes beside it: a finding suggesting a verb only the Examiner can call is dequeued by the Builder's beat and no longer closed by it.

Twelve tests on an invented domain cover the four relaxations, both caps, an atom no rule relaxes, an accepted proposal, one the pool turns down, and the closing rule. Measured offline over copies of both workdirs, spend zero, over the two rounds the cap allows. First corpus: 15 of 19 relaxed, over_strict 19 to 11, mean false rejection over the pooled Tasks 0.604 to 0.406, trusted 126 to 128. Second: all 6 relaxed, over_strict 6 to 5, mean 0.722 to 0.698, trusted unmoved at 39. Two numbers cut against the premise. Two trusted Tasks is nothing like a fix for the founder's count, because most over-strict Verifiers already failed another check. And in round two, eight Tasks on the first corpus and two on the second answer no rule relaxes write: the held-out Run reached the Reference End state while never calling the write tool, a shape neither the pool filter nor this step reads. The loophole probe could not be re-run offline, so those counts are an upper bound; a live round buys one probe Run per proposal.

### D206. A shape atom is checked against what the Run read, not against the row it writes (2026-09-09)

The ask is to observe from the errors and fix generally, without overfitting. The error is a Verifier rejecting its own Reference. On the first corpus 49 Tasks stopped at the D79 suite, and 17 of the 20 oracle failures and all 17 second-path failures with a real second path were one D190 shape atom: it demanded a system_derived write value already sit under its column in the row the write acts on, while the value was genuine and read off a result, only off another row, an ordinary copy across rows.

The rule. A system_derived value is one the Candidate read for itself, so the shape is checked against the Run and not only against the row: a result the Run received under that column before the write, off any call and any row, satisfies it, and so does a prose result stating it as a token, as a D203 reader answers. The world at large is still not searched, so a value the Run never read is still rejected. The widening is spent only where needed: the derivation asks the rule which source accepts its own Reference and stores no more than that rung, so a Task the row rule already accepted keeps D190's rule. Beside that, an empty column holds nothing rather than the emptiness back, a shape no source accepts is a derivation defect and is dropped as shape_self_reject rather than stored, and a tool result is now a turn of its own in all three transcript builders, which moved two frozen files, verifier_suite.py and runner/atom_context.py.

A re-roll's Run id also gained the key it was rolled under, because a re-roll replaced a Task's Runs under a name that did not move and a later round wrote new bytes under it. On a static copy three Tasks point a write atom at a call their file no longer holds, every re-roll file is younger than the Verifier reading it, and a fourth is gone.

Nine tests on an invented domain cover the four sources, a value never read, an empty column, the self-reject and a swap the row rule turns away.

Measured offline over copies of both workdirs, spend zero. First corpus, 172 confirmed Tasks: oracle failures 15 to 1 and second-path failures with a real second path 13 to 1, the one left in each a policy constraint, not a shape; the suite passes whole on 123 then 135, trusted 119 then 129, none lost; shapes 308 own row, 58 prior result, 30 no column, two dropped. Second corpus, 62 Tasks: oracle failures 4 to 0, trusted 38 either way. No check newly fails anywhere.

Three numbers cut against the premise. The report counted 20 and 17; on a static copy they are 15 and 13, because later rounds moved Tasks. Trusted moves 10, not 17, because blocked Tasks fail other checks behind the shape. And the second corpus never uses the widening: every shape there is satisfied by the row, and its four fixes come from the empty-column reading alone. A first version widening every atom let the wrong Run through on five Tasks and cost four trusted, which is what the rung rule answers.

Next builds: whether the two dropped shapes are a class, and the constraint behind the last oracle failure.

### D208. A derived artefact is retired when its source is withdrawn (2026-09-09)

The founder's ask is to observe from the errors and fix generally, without overfitting (2026-09-08). The error read as a held-out Run that reached the Reference End state while never calling the write tool. Every reproduced case sits on a Task whose reference_confirmed is False this round, with a Verifier file from an earlier round still under verifiers/. D198's residue path can withdraw a Reference a previous round confirmed and leaves what it produced on disk; the readers of over_strict then score that file against a pool since grown with Runs from the disagreement itself.

The rule. A Verifier carries the identity of the Reference it was derived from, the Run ids of the group it was derived over, and a confirmed status row now names those same ids. It is live only while its Task's row confirms that same Reference. Withdrawn and it is retired under reference_withdrawn; replaced by another group and under reference_rederived; a Reference that merely grew a Run is the same source and retires nothing. Retirement is a step of the derivation: the file leaves verifiers/, the row says it was retired and why, and the version goes into history unaccepted, so it stays readable and cannot come back by name, and only deriving again gives the Task a Verifier. Every consumer, the report included, reads the live set through one accessor on the Examiner's plan, so no gate picks a file off disk. A Task without a live Verifier reports verifier_retired with its reason rather than over_strict, and the false-rejection finding it had is closed. The rule is about lineage: anything derived from a Reference that records the Runs behind it is retired by the same accessor. D199's synthesised paths and D201's transactions hold no such identity on disk, so neither needs a row today.

A row served from the derivation's cache was written before the artefact was retired, so the marker is carried onto it while the Task still has nothing on disk and dropped as soon as a fresh derivation puts a Verifier back; without that, a later round forgets every earlier round's retirement. Twelve tests on an invented domain cover both reasons, a Reference that grew, a derivation retiring one Task while another keeps its own, the history row it leaves, a cached row an earlier retirement is carried onto, and a fresh confirmation deriving afresh.

Measured offline over copies of both workdirs, code only, spend zero. First: 34 of 206 retired, held-out Runs rejected 78 to 10, over_strict 24 to 9, false-rejection failures 24 to 9, trusted 122 to 122. Second: 23 of 85 retired, held-out rejected 67 to 4, over_strict 8 to 3, failures 8 to 3, trusted 38 to 38. Two numbers cut against the premise. The reading behind this expected three orphaned Tasks on the first corpus and two on the second; they are 34 and 23, of which 15 and 5 were ruled over-strict, so the class is several times what the end-state question reached. And trusted moves by nothing, because an orphaned Verifier's row already fails verifier_passed and was never trusted; what this buys is the measurement and the loosening driven off it.

Read verifiers_retired and its two reasons per round, beside trusted and over_strict. Both workdirs predate the Run ids on the row, so only the withdrawal half applied to them; reference_rederived counts from the next derivation.
### D209. Every Task carries an objective difficulty record, and trusted coverage is reported per bucket (2026-09-09)

The founder asked for a knob that changes how hard the Tasks are as training proceeds, after reading how an online task-synthesis pipeline keeps each task in a learnable band against the newest checkpoint. The standing ask holds, to observe from the errors and fix generally without overfitting. Kullback does not train, but its trusted Tasks are what a customer trains on, and a build reports one count with no notion of what kind of Task it holds: 127 of 209 on one live workdir, 38 of 122 on the second, 0 of 329 on the third, none saying whether those trusted write nothing or write three times. Nothing can be shifted until that count is broken out, so this is the measurement half.

The rule. For every Task with a derived Verifier the harness computes a record from what it already stores, code only: the write atoms of the live Verifier, the tools the Reference Run called, the rewrites of that call path the second-path rule finds with its cap off, the further ways to the End state the derivation confirmed, the question atoms, the share of the held-out pool the Verifier accepts, and the judge agreement over the rows naming the Task. Both rates are None on an empty pool and where no row names the Task: an unread rate is not a perfect one. A bucket is a triple of fixed bands, writes 0, 1, 2 or 3 and more, tools the same, paths 1 or 2 and more. difficulty.json holds the record and the bucket per Task, and the Tasks with neither under the harness's own reason, never the status row's, which quotes the customer's world back. The Verifiers it reads are the live ones, through D208's accessor rather than off disk, so a Verifier retired with the Reference it was derived from is not read as a Task's difficulty and that Task carries the harness's own reason for having none. The round line gains trusted over Tasks per bucket, the report a table of Tasks, trusted and mean solve rate per bucket, and a read command prints that table for a finished build. Counts land in the driver and the table in its own file, since the round-end module and the trust gate are frozen.

Eighteen tests on an invented greenhouse cover the record, the bands either side of every cap, the round line, the report table, an empty pool reading as no rate, a Verifier whose Reference was withdrawn, one derived over a Run the Reference no longer names, a Task the retirement step already took the file from, and a workdir end to end.

Measured offline over copies of three workdirs, spend zero. First: 205 records, 8 without, trusted 21 of 22, 66 of 74, 24 of 28, 15 of 23 and 1 of 1. Second: 85 records, 38 without, 17 of 21, 10 of 10, 5 of 5, 2 of 2, 2 of 3 and 1 of 1. Third: 15 records, 314 without, trusted nowhere. Two numbers cut against the premise. Writes separate weakly, 95, 89, 86 and 65 percent trusted as the count rises on the first corpus, while paths separate absolutely, every single-path Task untrusted, 54, 38 and 9 of them, so that band may read the second-path check and not difficulty. And a table is only as wide as the Verifiers: on the third it covers 15 of 329 Tasks. Next is the write and tool bands on a corpus with Verifiers for most Tasks.

### D210. The Simulated user ends by protocol and answers from typed facts (2026-09-09)

The founder's standing ask is to fix generally, without overfitting. A read of a published evaluation user gave the shape: facts disclosed only once asked, an ending that is a protocol. Kullback's facts were already right, mined from the recorded user's own turns and never invented (D44, D77), but it ended on a closing-cue regex plus a silence counter. So no report could tell a Run that ended on a met goal from one that ended with the user out of things to say, and the false-rejection pool counted both as Runs that did the Task: 553 of 600 re-rolls on the first corpus and 250 of 264 on the second close that way, with nothing to separate them. And a question the user held no fact for was answered by reading the Starting state aloud, the one way a value only the system knew reaches a Candidate through the user.

The rule. Every end carries one of four kinds. goal_satisfied is every write the goal implies confirmed made, those writes being the write-kind tools the Reference itself called and the world did not refuse. scenario_exhausted is the Candidate asking twice for what this user has no record of, or the user having nothing left to say. handed_off is the Candidate closing or passing the conversation on. gave_up is the turn limit, which the user never sees, so it is read off the Runner's reason. The cue and the counter are inputs to a kind, never the end alone, so a Candidate that says goodbye over a half-made goal is answered rather than agreed with. Facts are typed: a value a user of this Task said is askable and is spoken once asked, a value only the world holds is a record fact the user points at without naming. Every value the user speaks goes through D196's strip, prepared once per Task, and a value it removes is answered as absent and counted. No model call is added, and the kind reaches the Run through the turn's tags, which the loop already copies, so no frozen file moved.

Nineteen tests on an invented domain reach each kind by its own path and cover the typed answers and the strip. Measured offline over copies of both workdirs, spend zero. First corpus, 600 re-rolls: 409 goal_satisfied, 145 scenario_exhausted, 26 handed_off, 14 gave_up, 6 unclassified. Second, 264: 49, 198, 10, 7. The pool is where this pays: the first admits 569 held-out Runs of which 146 did not finish, 27 Tasks of 195 with nothing finished in their pool; the second admits 250 of which 198 did not finish, 54 Tasks of 86. Two numbers cut against the premise. The strip caught nothing, 0 of 864 Runs, because a mined fact is already limited to what a user said and the strip removes only what no user said; the leak lived in the world fallback, which the classes close, 246 record questions on the first corpus and 25 on the second. And no pool membership or trusted ruling moves offline, because the pool admits on the termination reason alone and nothing rules on a kind yet. Read on the next build whether goal_satisfied tracks the trusted count. Review on the pull request found one hole in the protocol and it is closed here: a turn whose only content was that this user has no record of what was asked counted as the user having spoken, so the close was never evaluated and a Candidate that only ever asked for unknown fields ran to the turn limit and read as gave_up. The four kinds are now decided in one place, reached from that turn as well as from the turn that asked nothing by name, so the counts above were taken before the hole was closed and the split moves toward scenario_exhausted by however many Runs died that way. The same review named a second hole that is not closed here: a write the world refused is in the transcript under its own name with the refusal dropped, so goal_satisfied can count it, and carrying the outcome into the transcript changes what every Candidate reads and wants its own decision.

### D211. A stalled body changes strategy: every differing leaf, the relation across failing calls, and the lines nothing witnesses (2026-09-09)

The founder's ask stands: observe from the errors and fix things generally, it should not overfit. Eleven kept tool bodies across three workdirs replay under half their evidence calls, one at 0.105 and one at 0.000, each after twelve to thirty one recompiles that scored no higher. Keeping the incumbent on a tie is right, but nothing changed what the writer was shown or asked when it stalled: a hint per call and one leaf deep, a few masked failure shapes, a sentence saying the tool had stalled. Nothing looked across a tool's failing calls for the rule they share, and nothing said which lines of the body no recorded call reaches.

The rule. A new module, kullback/builder/lesson.py, holds four code only steps and their constants, each keyed on structure alone: how two answers are shaped, what the arguments hold, what the recording did. First, the full diff: a failing call's line lists every leaf the two answers part on, path and both sides, capped, with the count left out, built from the recorded result, the sandbox answer and the arguments, since the per call rows keep only a digest. Second, a relation catalogue over one tool's failing calls, five kinds: broadcast, one element's value carried onto every element; shared value, one side answering a run of calls or positions alike where the other tells them apart; a refusal predicate the refused calls share and the accepted lack; arithmetic, a recorded number that is a sum, difference, product or count over the call's own numbers; order, a recorded list in a sort the body did not make. One holding on every failing call and on none the body answers is stated with counts, one holding on most with its exceptions. Third, the witnessed branch read, a lesson input and no gate: the sandbox traces which lines each recorded call ran, and a branch, loop or assignment nothing reached is listed as unsupported, a behaviour the recording shows and the body has no line for as missing. Fourth, the stall limit: past six unbeaten recompiles the ask becomes a rewrite from the recorded calls rather than a patch, the whole failing set replaces a sample, and a tie where both bodies fall at a gate before the fidelity ruling is reported as blocked by that named gate. Counted per round: relations by kind, unwitnessed lines, rewrites forced, blocked by gate. Prose is read into columns by the tool's own reader first, and the held out split stays out of the lesson.

Thirty five tests on an invented depot domain cover every relation kind found and absent, the capped diff, an unreached branch, a missing refusal, the switched ask and a blocked tie. Measured offline on copies of three workdirs, code only, spend zero: eleven rewrites forced, five unwitnessed constructs on three tools, four missing refusals, two relations, both shared value, one on all failing calls and one on 119 of 123. Three numbers cut against the premise. Broadcast, order, arithmetic and refusal predicate fire on none of the eleven, whose bodies read the wrong row rather than hold a wrong rule and whose refusals turn on the world, not on an argument class. The diff buys nothing on six of eleven, parting at one leaf, and much on the rest, mean 14.4 leaves and maximum 42. The wider failing set buys almost nothing: the gates cap their failure list at five sentences before the shaper sees it, so what widens is the diff. One tool of sixty five is blocked by a gate. Read the next relaunch for the trusted effect, which no offline run can show.

### D212. Every sample the harness draws per Task or Run is a function of the id and a build salt (2026-09-09)

The founder's ask is to observe from the errors and fix things generally, without overfitting (2026-09-08). What was observed is a moving measurement, not a failing Task: several draws were functions of a count or a position. On the first workdir, 209 Tasks, the bounded probe budget changed hands for 70 Tasks when the same Tasks arrived in another order, and on the second, 122 Tasks, for 66. The anchor picked held-out Runs by position in each Task's sorted Run list, so one Run appearing moved the held-out set of 35 of 72 guarded Tasks and of 14 of 23. The second-path search restarted its batch counter at one on every call, writing over what an earlier search left.

The rule. One function, sample_key of a kind, an id and a build salt, returns a stable 64 bit value from a content hash of the three, and every seed, share, pick and order comes from it. The salt is written into the workdir on the first draw and read back after, so a resume draws what the first launch drew; it defaults to one constant, so two workdirs of one Environment stay comparable. Held-out membership is a threshold on each Run's own key, filled and trimmed to the exact share D81 owes. A bounded budget goes out in key order, not list order. A count that must grow takes higher attempt indexes and never reshuffles those taken, and a Run made again under its own id draws the seed of the Run it replaced. Each round records the salt's first eight characters and its draws by kind.

Twenty six tests on an invented domain: stability across invocations, independence when an id joins or leaves, the salt stored once and reused, a re-made Run reusing its seed, the budget indifferent to Task order, batch numbers above the ones bought.

Measured on copies of both Task lists, code only, spend zero. Probe membership moving on a reorder, 70 of 209 to 0 and 66 of 122 to 0. Anchor sets moving when a Run joins each guarded Task, 35 of 72 to 17 and 14 of 23 to 6. Three things cut against the premise. The anchor cannot reach zero while D81 owes an exact share of a Run list that grew, so what the key buys is that the mover is the new Run's own rank. The re-roll seed the reading blamed was not drifting: the stage pinned it to zero. And D133's split is a filter that draws nothing, while the judges are chosen by rule, so neither is keyed here.

Read sample_salt and the draws counts per round; two builds whose sample_salt differs are not comparable draw for draw.

### D213. When a Task's own Runs disagree on a row, each Run replays against its own sighting (2026-09-09)

The founder asked to observe from the errors and fix things generally, without overfitting (2026-09-08). Sixteen of twenty-four failing calls read across four tools on two corpora were one class. A Task groups several Runs, the Starting state pinned one row per id from the earliest sighting across all of them, and every call of the Task was scored on that version, including the call whose own recording answered with another. The bodies were not wrong: one indexed the world by a single direct key and still failed, on the world another Run saw. The counter meant to see this compared only reads, so a Run whose only mention of a row sat inside its own write's result was never compared.

The rule. What one Run saw of a row is read from every result that Run recorded, read or write, at any depth, so a nested sighting of a row something states plainly is marked rather than dropped. A Task overlay holds, per row and column, only what every Run that sighted the row agrees on. A column its Runs disagree on leaves the Task overlay and is carried by one layer per Run holding that Run's own earliest value, laid over the Task's rows when that Run replays. A Run that never sighted the column, and a generated Candidate Run, are served the Reference Run's layer whole rather than a mix of two; the Reference Run is the Task's first the Builder may build from. The sighting sequence is a within-Run statement and is built per Run. The per-call world the scoring path hashes is the calling Run's layer, so a change in either layer changes the hash. Every disagreement goes to the pinner's ruling with the table, the key class, the column classes and the Run ids, counted per round, and a Task whose Runs part on a column naming a row it writes is flagged a split candidate and filed as a finding, since the grouping may hold two Tasks.

Nine tests on an invented delivery network cover both Runs confirming against their own value, an agreed column staying with the Task, a write-only sighting counting, a Run nothing recorded served the Reference Run's world, the per-call hash parting, and the split flag.

Measured on copies, code only, spend zero. The first corpus holds no disagreement and stands at 193 of 205 Tasks and 417 of 456 Runs confirmed, one failing call fewer. The second holds one disagreeing column in one of 122 Tasks and does not move: 54 Tasks, 82 Runs, 353 failing calls either way. The third holds 104 rows and 537 columns across 91 of its 183 Tasks and moves from 34 to 37 Tasks, 45 to 61 Runs, and 2324 to 1795 failing calls. Nothing regressed.

Two numbers cut against the reading this was built from. The two Tasks it named no longer exist, the mining having regrouped, and the successor of the one whose Runs were said to disagree holds two that agree everywhere; on the second corpus the class is one column and the gain comes wholly from the third. And no corpus has a split candidate, so the flag has zero instances today. Read the four counters in the pinner's ruling on the next build, beside the failing calls per tool.

### D214. The Simulated user is an agent of the harness: its own package, curated context, guards, fidelity score and lesson loop (2026-09-09)

The founder said the Simulated user is the weakest component and that we need agentic user simulations made "just like the builder agent and examiner agent" and "grounded in the context we curate for it". This replaces the older fallback-only plan for D214, which would have left the rules primary and put a model behind them. The evidence for going further is the pool: D210 counted 198 re-rolls of 264 on one corpus ending because the user ran out of scenario, and 81 Tasks across two corpora whose held-out pool holds only Runs that never finished, and that pool decides whether a Verifier is over-strict.

The user is a package beside the Builder and the Examiner on the same agent core. The rule-driven user moved into it whole with a re-export left behind, the vocabulary split so its shape lives with the user and the mining with the Builder, and a contract holds that direction. Each Task gets a context curated from its recording and nothing else, every item a section under a stable tag: the typed facts with their askable and record classes, the goal in the recorded user's own words, a persona kept as counted classes rather than quotes, the write arguments the Reference chose, the conversation so far and last round's lessons. Its tools show what a person on the phone can see, and a consult tool appears only where a recording shows that user reading out a value only the world's records hold.

Code decides everything after the model. A value-shaped token that is not one of this user's own facts drops the turn, a record fact becomes the sentence that points at it, the D196 strip runs, and a turn volunteering more than the recorded user had by the same point is cut back, counted in facts so a Candidate cannot farm them by asking. The end kind is code's and the model may only request one. Every dropped turn is answered by the rule-driven user, which is also the floor: both drivers are scored offline against the recorded turns on the same facts carried, none added, no record fact spoken and the same ending, and the model drives only the Tasks it beats. What it got wrong becomes that Task's lesson next round, and a Task it has not beaten after three rounds stops paying until its facts or persona change.

Thirty five tests on an invented domain cover the imports, the mined classes, each guard, the end protocol, the driver choice, the fallback, the score, the lesson and stall rules, and the two CLI verbs.

Measured on copies and read-only workdirs. The rule-driven baseline per corpus is 0.822 over 203 Tasks, 0.798 over 88 and 0.840 over 19, with 12, 3 and 6 Tasks at 1.0. Live on the second corpus with the contributor model, first four Tasks in id order: the agent means 0.785 against 0.779 and beats the rules on three of the four, for 0.0060 USD. A wall-clock cap cut it there, and a twelve Task run stopped at 0.0045 USD having scored under two, since a turn costs about twenty five seconds on this model.

Three things cut against the premise. The dry-run count that motivated the decision cannot be read off any of the three workdirs: all 942 stored Runs predate D210's kinds, so what is there is termination reasons and not ends. The rule-driven baseline is higher than the premise implies, so this score reads turns and not whether a Run finished. And the agent's margin is thin, 0.006 of a mean over four Tasks, so what the driver choice buys stays unmeasured until a build launched with a user model reads ends by kind and runs_recovered over the held-out re-rolls.

### D215. A write is judged on every row it changed, not only on the answer it returned (2026-09-09)

The founder's standing ask is to observe from the errors and fix generally without overfitting (2026-09-08). A write was judged on whether its result matched the recorded result, and nothing else. A tool that debits a balance, appends to a history or recomputes a total out of two catalogue rows does all of it inside itself, and a body returning the right answer while moving none of it passes the write check outright. The shortfall surfaces only if a later read happens to look, and is then blamed on the read; otherwise the Task confirms on a wrong world. The recording holds 3058 such columns over 569 write calls on the first corpus and 736 over 144 on the second, none checked.

The evidence was there all along. A Run reads a row, writes, and later reads something carrying it again; what sits between two differing sightings is the write. `builder/effects.py` reads that from any two sightings, whatever call carried them, takes the Task's Starting state as the before value when nothing read the row first, drops exempt columns by the miner's name rule and the schema's exempt class, and keeps the rows, paths, values on either side, and whether one write or several sat between. It has three readers. The body prompt gains an effects section per write tool listing the columns each recorded call changed on rows its arguments never name, with before and after and, for numbers, the relation `lesson.arithmetic_relations` finds over the named rows and the rows the Run had read, given by table and path and never by value; where two calls agree it states the rule, where they disagree it shows both. The memorised gate refuses a body writing down an after value it should have computed, as D187 rule (d) refuses one copied off a result. The replay compares each credited column against the world after the write, failing it with kind `effect` naming table and path and marking the read that saw the stale value `downstream_of` it. Only the replay sees held-out Runs, being code that tells no one anything.

Twenty tests on an invented lending domain. The first cut checked an ambiguously credited column after every write credited with it, which is unsound: the recording says where a column ended only once every write between the sightings has run. Such a column is now checked after the last credited write.

Measured read only on trimmed copies, before against after. First corpus: Tasks confirmed 207 to 207 of 213, References 454 to 454, writes matched 562 to 562 of 583, effect checks 0 to 2236, failures 96, downstream 22. Second corpus: Tasks confirmed 89 to 88 of 125, References 138 to 135, writes matched 127 to 120 of 176, effect checks 0 to 424, failures 16. The unsound cut cost 207 to 157 and 89 to 80. Recompiling the first corpus's seven write tools with the evidence in the prompt left every body byte identical, two because the redraft scored below the body already there, so the replay repeated exactly; 0.08 USD on the fallback model, the build model having exhausted its retries on read timeouts.

Two disagreements. The brief expected fidelity to fall because the check is stricter, and on the first corpus it does not move: every failing column landed on a write already failing its result check. And prose results are not homed, so a tool answering in a sentence yields nothing. Next builds should read why a redraft carrying the evidence still loses to the body it must beat, and whether the second corpus's newly failing writes are body faults or mis-credits.

### D216. The split of Runs into Tasks is a function of the recordings alone (2026-09-09)

The founder asked for fixes read off the errors that hold generally (2026-09-08). The error is the drift D200 made visible and left open: the third corpus's frozen list holds 183 Tasks while its round mined 329, only 110 carrying a frozen Run set and 73 never re-mined, and the other two drift 205 to 209 and 119 to 122 with no new recording. The cause sat in the split by starting world, where a row's version was hashed over the mined schema's hard columns, a generated reader's readings of a sentence, and the rows the readers stage merged in. All three are the harness's own proposals, so every reader written and every column reclassified regrouped it: removing the readers artifact alone moved the third corpus from 329 mined Tasks to 195.

The rule is that a row sighting's version is a function of the recordings and of nothing else: the canonical form of the recorded result projected to that row, filed under the tool that answered together with the row's identity. Sightings through different tools are never compared, so a list projection and a detail projection of one row no longer split a Task; one trace contradicts another only where the same tool showed the same row in two versions before either wrote. A prose result homed onto a row is its own recorded sentence, and another requestor's tools are read the same way, keyed by the requestor, so merge_worlds is gone. Row identity stays mined, itself a function of the recordings. The clustering records a fingerprint of its Run sets beside the recording set and homing hashes; a round whose fingerprint differs names which input moved, and one that differs while both stood still raises. A frozen_only Task is re-examined each round, losing the flag where the recordings would still group its Runs and otherwise carrying the split's reason, tool and row named. The round line gains tasks_cleared and tasks_added_cost.

Fourteen tests on invented domains cover byte identical worlds under every column class and a bumped cache format, two projections of one row not splitting, a prose read splitting with no reader, the fingerprint's raise, and a frozen_only Task clearing.

Measured on copies of three live workdirs, code only, spend zero. Mined Tasks go 209 to 205, 122 to 119, 329 to 230; matched to the frozen list by Run set 201 to 205, 116 to 119, 110 to 149; frozen_only 4 to 0, 3 to 0, 73 to 34; added stays 0 and published stays 205, 119, 183. The fingerprint is byte identical on each corpus across the plain run, the run with the readers artifact removed and the run with the cache format bumped. The remaining 34 all carry a reason, on three device tools.

Three numbers cut against the premise. The brief expected the recordings alone to split more than the mined view did; the opposite happened everywhere, filing by tool removing more splits than dropping the class filter adds. The clearing half measured zero, the third corpus's other 39 having been reproduced outright, so it ships tested and unseen live. And added is 0 everywhere, so tasks_added_cost is 0 and is a share of the per-Task stages. No frozen file moved.

### D217. A cosmetic verdict needs the same values on both sides (2026-09-09)

The founder's ask stands: observe from the errors and fix things generally, it should not overfit. On the largest write of one workdir a replay reported one device state where the recording reported another, the verdict was cosmetic, and the Trace counted as confirmed. Where neither the bytes nor the canonical string matched, a comparer walked the two answers over the union of their keys, so a column one side alone held read as null on the other, and the class then forgave it: exempt is equal whatever it holds, and semantic goes to the judge or, with no judge given, stays unresolved, neither recording a failure. The hard set came back empty, and cosmetic counts as agreement. Before the change 2211 of 13536 replayed calls in one workdir, 77 of 3304 in a second and 23 of 1596 in a third agreed that way, with nothing saying which of them rested on the bytes.

The rule. Two differences are never cosmetic, whatever the column's class. The first is presence: a value one side holds and the other does not fails, an exempt column included and a semantic one without asking the judge, and the only exception is a value the canonical form calls empty on both sides. The second is the domain tokens two values carry. A domain token is one of the names the world uses for a column: for a column the corpus showed drawing from a small set of short repeated names, its own set, and for a free text column the names any column of its table draws from, because a sentence about a row names that row's states in the world's own words. The judge is asked whether two sentences mean the same only once both are written out of the same names; where the sets differ the verdict is differs with the reason token_set naming the tokens on each side, and the judge is not asked, so the cost falls. A world that lent no names leaves the comparison as it was. The miner records that set per column now, since a few kept samples say what a column looks like and not what it may hold; an id column and a column whose every sighting was its own value lend nothing. Every verdict records its route, the round counts each, a reason names the route, and the stage's cache key carries a verdict format so older verdicts recompute.

Nineteen tests on invented kiln and lamp domains: a state on one side only failing an exempt column, both sides empty passing, equal token sets reaching the judge and differing ones skipping it, a name inside a longer word not counting, the route on each verdict, the counters on the round, the mined vocabulary.

Measured offline on three workdirs, replay only, no model call, spend zero. The first fell from 115 Tasks confirmed of 406 to 53, none gained, its cosmetic verdicts from 2211 to 677, and 1656 calls part by token set, every sample of which read by hand was two genuinely different states. The other two did not move, 207 of 213 and 89 of 125, since neither schema classes a column semantic, so no Task of either rested on a judged cosmetic verdict. Three readings cut against the premise. The typed facts of D210 do not know which columns are enumerated, so the vocabulary is mined here and is new. The judge is not what masked the largest workdir: none is handed to the replay comparer, so a semantic column was forgiven for being unresolved, and 387 calls still agree that way. And it ran on the samples an older schema kept rather than the mined set, so the token sets are partial and the effect grows at the next mine.

### D219. A comparison nobody settled is not agreement (2026-09-09)
